only when existing tools cannot express the constraint reliably.\nSeparate formatting from design checks so cosmetic changes cannot conceal an architectural failure.\nAvoid arbitrary file, function or rule-count limits without a project-specific reason.\n\n## Example mappings\n\n| Principle | Concrete rule | Enforcement | Required evidence |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Keep persistence behind one interface | UI modules cannot import the database adapter | Import boundary analysis resolving project paths and aliases | An allowed public interface import passes and a direct database import fails |\n| Write each rule once | Generated settings match the authoritative source | Deterministic regeneration and comparison | A stale generated value fails |\n| Validate external input | Reject invalid input at the actual public boundary | Behaviour tests and applicable schema or type checks | Invalid input is rejected and valid input succeeds |\n| Keep behaviour stable during cleanup | Existing supported journeys remain compatible | Relevant integration or end-to-end checks | The same supported journeys pass before and after the change |\n| Keep dependencies purposeful | New abstractions serve a current use and a clear responsibility | Human design review against vision and technical design | Review identifies the requirement, responsibility and simpler alternative |\n\nDo not disguise a human design judgement as a deterministic violation.\nGive the reviewer the relevant authority, example and evidence instead.\n\n## Proof required before adopting an automated rule\n\nRequire a representative valid case and a deliberate violation in disposable fixtures.\nThe valid case must pass.\nThe violation must fail with the expected diagnostic and exit code.\nThe clean project must pass the selected final checks.\nUse the selected tool's native rule-testing mechanism where available.\nInclude relevant syntax, aliases, re-exports, generated code and documented exceptions in the proof scope.\nRecord unsupported syntax and dynamic dependencies as coverage limits rather than assuming they pass.\n\nCapture the maintained command, tool version, output, exit code and relevant input state directly from execution.\nThe same maintained command must work locally and in the project's CI when the project selects one, without ignoring failures.\nFor behaviour compatibility, preserve agreed acceptance outcomes rather than proving only that unit checks are green.\nThese are evidence requirements for selected enforcement, not a development workflow.\nThis reference supplies guidance only.\nThe early reference checkpoint does not implement rule-proof, baseline handling or maintenance validation.\n\n## Control existing debt\n\nFix selected findings and keep the remaining findings visible.\nWhen immediate cleanup is unsuitable, propose a narrow record of known violations for the user to accept as temporary debt.\nPrefer the native tool's baseline or suppression mechanism where it exists.\nNew violations must fail, and fixed entries must leave the debt record so it shrinks as fixes land.\nDo not silently refresh a baseline to accept new failures.\nAn exception must record its reason, scope and removal condition.\nNever disable a whole rule or exclude a whole directory merely to obtain a green result.\nA changed-file-only check cannot establish a constraint that depends on the complete import graph or generated outputs.\n\n## One authoritative source per rule\n\nPreserve an existing standards source that already serves reviewers.\nMissing `CODING_STANDARDS.md` is not a readiness failure.\nCreate that document only when non-mechanical review rules need a distinct maintained home.\nAn existing design section can suffice for a small project.\nKeep review standards actionable and scoped, with a reason, example and exception policy where needed.\n\nTool configurations own formatting values and executable constraints.\nStandards link to those checks rather than copying settings.\nAgent instructions point to the relevant documents and commands while preserving distinct scopes.\nIf a selected edit moves a review rule, replace its old text with a link to the new authority.\nDesign documents may explain the structural decision without maintaining a second copy of the standard.\nRead relevant existing decisions before proposing a conflicting rule.\nKeep proposals as drafts until selected and preserve review before apply for documentation as well as code.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

[isolated-home]/.agents/skills/repo-audit/references/architecture.md'","aggregated_output":"# Architecture\n\nUse this reference when recommending module boundaries.\nStart with the reported area, stated next change, approved design and relevant history.\nExpand only where evidence leads.\nFor a new project, review expected changes against proposed boundaries instead of inventing history.\n\nCoupling means that a change needs knowledge of, or edits to, another part.\nCohesion means that code belongs together for a clear responsibility.\nA module is a logical unit with an interface and implementation, which may span several private files.\nA god file mixes unrelated responsibilities and becomes a shared target for different changes.\nLength alone does not establish that problem.\n\n## Principles to consider\n\nChoose principles only where project evidence supports them.\nNo single architecture style is required.\n\n| Principle | Practical rule | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Single responsibility and cohesion | Keep code together when it changes for the same business reason and separate unrelated reasons | [Single responsibility](https://blog.cleancoder.com/uncle-bob/2014/05/08/SingleReponsibilityPrinciple.html) |\n| Information hiding | Hide a changeable design decision behind an interface so callers do not depend on its representation | [Module decomposition](https://www.cs.lafayette.edu/~gexia/cs301/resources/parnas.html) |\n| Locality of change | Keep capability code and tests together where this reduces coordination across unrelated areas | [Vertical slices](https://www.jimmybogard.com/vertical-slice-architecture/) |\n| Explicit dependency direction | Define allowed dependencies and check forbidden imports and cycles across intended boundaries | [Dependency hierarchy](https://www.cs.lafayette.edu/~gexia/cs301/resources/parnas.html) |\n| Separate wiring from behaviour | Startup assembles modules while modules own business behaviour | [Composition roots](https://blog.ploeh.dk/2011/07/28/CompositionRoot/) |\n| Proportional deployment choices | A single deployed application can retain firm module boundaries | [Module boundaries](https://www.martinfowler.com/articles/microservice-trade-offs.html) |\n\nA deep module hides useful complexity behind a small interface.\nConsolidate tightly related shallow modules when removing their layer concentrates complexity in one place.\nSeparate unrelated responsibilities when their changes currently require shared edits.\nAvoid forwarding layers that add navigation without hiding useful complexity.\nOrganise ownership around domain knowledge rather than scattering one rule across load, validate and save phases.\nThese adaptations follow the pinned design and module guidance attributed in the package NOTICE.\n\n## What the audit inspects\n\n- Unrelated business responsibilities, or startup, UI, domain rules and persistence combined without clear internal boundaries.\n- Imports of private implementation, leaked representations and cycles across intended module boundaries.\n- Shared mutable state and multiple writers of canonical data.\n- Frequently changed files and pairs that repeatedly change together.\n- Central registries, broad utilities, shared type collections and wiring files that unrelated work repeatedly edits.\n- Tests requiring unrelated modules or asserting implementation details rather than a public contract.\n- Two representative planned changes, their write paths and the shared contracts they would modify.\n\nCollect dependency edges through the native analysis selected by the language research brief.\nResolve aliases, re-exports and package entry points.\nReport unsupported syntax, dynamic dependencies and unresolved imports as coverage limits.\nUnresolved imports do not demonstrate independence.\nPrefer an existing architecture checker to another dependency engine.\n\nFor history, record revision range, rename handling and exclusions for generated files, lockfiles and broad formatting changes.\nShow the supporting commits behind a finding.\nCo-change is distinct from observed merge conflicts.\nHistory may omit abandoned rebases and conflict resolutions.\n\n## Signals that are not violations\n\nSize, import counts and change frequency indicate where to investigate.\nThey do not prove a bad boundary.\nA large cohesive domain owner may be sound.\nA thin startup file may legitimately import many modules for wiring.\nA stable domain contract may have many callers.\nCo-change may reflect good cohesion, a mechanical commit or harmful coupling.\nInspect the reasons before recommending a split or consolidation.\nDo not infer conflicts or prescribe services from these signals.\n\n## Boundary rules\n\nName each module's responsibility, owned knowledge, public contract and private implementation.\nDefine allowed dependency direction and the scope of forbidden imports and cycles.\nCallers use the public interface and do not depend on private storage or representation.\nPrivate implementation may span files without exposing additional public entry points.\nKeep startup focused on assembly and put behaviour with its domain owner.\nRemove unnecessary shared write targets before serializing work.\nWhere sharing is real, enforce one writer and sequence unavoidable shared edits.\n\nReview two representative changes against proposed boundaries.\nFor example, a pricing-policy change and a notification-template change should remain inside their owners when the public contracts still suffice.\nCheck whether either also changes a shared type, registry or data representation.\nCompare declared paths and contract changes, then review semantic dependencies even when paths are disjoint.\nA script reports overlap evidence but cannot prove independence or predict every conflict.\nFewer unrelated shared edits are an expected benefit, not a measured reduction until actual evidence exists.\n\nRecord each proposed boundary's project-specific reason, source, scope, enforcement and exception policy.\nReuse existing design decisions and standards rather than duplicating their rules.\nPresent evidenced candidates and alternatives for selection before applying changes.\nVerification requires representative allowed and forbidden imports and compatible public behaviour, not file-count targets.\nThis reference does not implement native rules or maintenance checks.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Account for every submitted ID before reporting completion.', { tag: 'tracked-batch', text: 'Apply ' + selected.length + ' selected review items', element: event.currentTarget, data: { items: selected } });\\\"><label><input type=\\\"checkbox\\\" name=\\\"items\\\" value=\\\"R-03\\\" data-label=\\\"Preserve rollback behavior\\\" data-disposition=\\\"must-address\\\"> R-03 — Preserve rollback behavior</label><label><input type=\\\"checkbox\\\" name=\\\"items\\\" value=\\\"R-08\\\" data-label=\\\"Reuse the existing error surface\\\" data-disposition=\\\"must-address\\\"> R-08 — Reuse the existing error surface</label><button type=\\\"submit\\\">Queue selected items</button></form>\",\"The agent's tracked-batch completion receipt must give every submitted ID exactly one outcome: addressed with concrete evidence, deferred with a reason, or rejected with a reason. {"type":"item.started","item":{"id":"item_9","type":"command_execution","command":"[host-home]/.nix-profile/bin/zsh -lc \"cat > [scratch]/prepare.py <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json\np=Path('[scratch]/payloads')\nfiles={\n'GLOSSARY.md':'''# Pantry terms\n\n- **Item:** a named pantry supply.\n- **Shopping list:** items the household marks as needing to buy.\n- **Needed:** the household's mark that an item needs buying.\n''',\n'README.md':'''# Pantry list\n\nA local household pantry and shopping list, without accounts or stock tracking.\nSee [VISION.md](VISION.md), [GLOSSARY.md](GLOSSARY.md) and [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md).\n\n## Setup and use\n\nUse Node.js 24 and npm.\nRun \"'`npm ci`, then `npm run dev` and open http://127.0.0.1:5173.\nThe local development server serves browser files; there is no application backend.\nKeep that address and browser profile stable: local storage belongs to the origin.\nClearing browser data removes pantry records; this version has no backup or synchronization.\n\nAdd Rice, mark it needed, see Rice in Shopping list, then reload and retain it.\nUnchecking Needed removes it from the shopping list while retaining the pantry item.\n\n## Verification\n\nRun `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run compile` and `npm run build`.\nInstall the test browser with `npm run browser:install`, then run `npm test`.\n`npm run check` runs all checks and the real browser tests.\nBrowser tests use an isolated browser profile and a loopback static preview at port 4173.\nThey verify the Rice journey, unmarking, invalid names and storage failure handling.\nThere is no hosted CI integration or hosted execution claim.\n'\"''',\n'DESIGN.md':'''# Minimal foundation\n\n## Responsibilities and data flow\n\n\"'`src/main.ts` is the single browser entry: it wires form controls and renders pantry and shopping views.\n`src/list.ts` is the list module: it owns named items, needed marks, saved-data validation and persistence.\nThe entry calls the list module; only the list module reads and writes the pantry storage key.\nOne stored array is authoritative; the shopping list is derived from needed marks.\nA mutation validates and saves before publishing the new state to the UI.\nA failed save preserves the previous state and shows an error.\nMalformed or unreadable saved data is preserved and editing is blocked for that session.\nThere is no automatic destructive reset or recovery feature.\n\nNames are trimmed and required; case-insensitive duplicates are rejected and original spelling retained.\nNo quantity or stock-level field is part of the data model.\nStorage uses the versioned `pantry-list:v1` key and an array of `{ name, needed }` objects.\nThe runtime has no external API, secrets or remote services.\nVite is local static development/build tooling, not an application backend.\nUse a fixed loopback origin for reliable browser storage; file URL storage is not a supported setup.\n\n## Principles and review guidance\n\n- Household marking owns shopping needs. Source: VISION.md and the approved Rice journey. Scope: both source files. Reason: separately stored shopping state can drift. Enforce with the real browser journey and unmarking check. Quantity inference needs a new owner decision.\n- Persist before reporting success. Source: VISION.md reload commitment; proposed reliability guidance. Scope: list mutations and UI errors. Reason: silent failed writes break retention. Enforce with storage failure and invalid saved-data browser cases. No silent reset exception is allowed.\n- Keep the entry and list module focused. Source: this selected design. Scope: src. Reason: UI changes should not spread storage knowledge. Enforce by human review of contracts and storage access; TypeScript verifies types, not architecture. Any exception needs a reason, narrow scope and removal condition.\n- Native configurations own mechanical rules. Source: this selected foundation. Scope: source, tests and configs. Reason: duplicated prose and settings drift. Enforce using Prettier, ESLint, strict TypeScript, Vite and Playwright. Any suppression needs a reason, scope and removal condition; do not weaken checks merely to pass.\n\nUI label and layout changes belong to the entry and stylesheet.\nStorage-format changes belong to the list module and relevant failure tests.\nContract changes require review of both entry and list behaviour, not an assumption of file independence.\n\n## Acceptance and limits\n\nThe owner-approved first journey is add Rice, mark it needed, view Rice in the shopping list, reload and retain it.\nThe browser test uses visible controls and asserts state after a real page reload.\nBlank input, duplicate names, unmarking, failed writes and malformed saved data have separate acceptance checks proposed in this exact preview.\nCompile and unit-like checks alone do not establish the first journey.\nNative rule-proof, maintained enforcement and portable maintenance remain pending later work.\nNo accounts, quantities, application server or hosted CI are included.\n'\"''',\n'AGENTS.md':'''# Project guidance\n\nRead [VISION.md](VISION.md), [GLOSSARY.md](GLOSSARY.md) and [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md).\nUse [README.md](README.md) for local setup and the approved Rice journey.\nRun \"'`npm run check` after changes affecting the journey or foundation.\nRun `npm run format` to apply the configured formatter before exact review.\nKeep configuration as the source of executable rules; do not duplicate it here.\n'\"''',\n'.gitignore':'''node_modules/\ndist/\nplaywright-report/\ntest-results/\n''',\n'.prettierignore':'''node_modules/\ndist/\nplaywright-report/\ntest-results/\npackage-lock.json\n''',\n'.prettierrc.json':'{}\\\\n',\n'index.html':'''<\"'!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n  <head>\n    <meta charset=\"UTF-8\" />\n    <meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\" />\n    <title>Pantry list</title>\n  </head>\n  <body>\n    <main>\n      <h1>Pantry list</h1>\n      <form id=\"add-item\">\n        <label for=\"item-name\">Item name</label>\n        <input id=\"item-name\" name=\"item-name\" required autocomplete=\"off\" />\n        <button type=\"submit\">Add item</button>\n      </form>\n      <p id=\"message\" role=\"alert\"></p>\n      <section aria-labelledby=\"pantry-heading\">\n        <h2 id=\"pantry-heading\">Pantry</h2>\n        <ul id=\"pantry\"></ul>\n      </section>\n      <section aria-labelledby=\"shopping-heading\">\n        <h2 id=\"shopping-heading\">Shopping list</h2>\n        <ul id=\"shopping\"></ul>\n      </section>\n    </main>\n    <script type=\"module\" src=\"/src/main.ts\"></script>\n  </body>\n</html>\n'\"''',\n'src/list.ts':'''export interface Item {\n  readonly name: string;\n  readonly needed: boolean;\n}\n\nexport const STORAGE_KEY = \\\"pantry-list:v1\\\";\n\nfunction identity(name: string): string {\n  return name.toLowerCase();\n}\n\nfunction decode(raw: string | null): Item[] {\n  if (raw === null) return [];\n  const value: unknown = JSON.parse(raw);\n  if (\"'!Array.isArray(value)) throw new Error(\"Invalid saved pantry.\");\n  const names = new Set<string>();\n  return value.map((entry: unknown) => {\n    if (\n      typeof entry !== \"object\" ||\n      entry === null ||\n      !(\"name\" in entry) ||\n      !(\"needed\" in entry) ||\n      typeof entry.name !== \"string\" ||\n      entry.name.trim() === \"\" ||\n      entry.name !== entry.name.trim() ||\n      typeof entry.needed !== \"boolean\" ||\n      Object.keys(entry).length !== 2 ||\n      names.has(identity(entry.name))\n    ) {\n      throw new Error(\"Invalid saved pantry.\");\n    }\n    names.add(identity(entry.name));\n    return { name: entry.name, needed: entry.needed };\n  });\n}\n\nexport function openList(storage: Pick<Storage, \"getItem\" | \"setItem\">) {\n  let items = decode(storage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY));\n\n  function commit(next: Item[]): void {\n    try {\n      storage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(next));\n    } catch {\n      throw new Error(\"Could not save. Your previous pantry is unchanged.\");\n    }\n    items = next;\n  }\n\n  return {\n    items: (): readonly Item[] => items.map((item) => ({ ...item })),\n    add(name: string): void {\n      const trimmed = name.trim();\n      if (!trimmed) throw new Error(\"Enter an item name.\");\n      if (items.some((item) => identity(item.name) === identity(trimmed))) {\n        throw new Error(\"That item is already in the pantry.\");\n      }\n      commit([...items, { name: trimmed, needed: false }]);\n    },\n    markNeeded(name: string, needed: boolean): void {\n      if (!items.some((item) => item.name === name)) {\n        throw new Error(\"That item is no longer in the pantry.\");\n      }\n      commit(items.map((item) => (item.name === name ? { ...item, needed } : item)));\n    },\n  };\n}\n'\"''',\n'src/main.ts':'''import { openList } from \\\"./list\\\";\nimport \\\"./style.css\\\";\n\nfunction element<T extends HTMLElement>(selector: string): T {\n  const found = document.querySelector<T>(selector);\n  if (\"'!found) throw new Error(`Missing page element: ${selector}`);\n  return found;\n}\n\nconst form = element<HTMLFormElement>(\"#add-item\");\nconst input = element<HTMLInputElement>(\"#item-name\");\nconst message = element<HTMLParagraphElement>(\"#message\");\nconst pantry = element<HTMLUListElement>(\"#pantry\");\nconst shopping = element<HTMLUListElement>(\"#shopping\");\n\nfunction start(): void {\n  let list: ReturnType<typeof openList>;\n  try {\n    list = openList(window.localStorage);\n  } catch {\n    message.textContent =\n      \"Could not read saved pantry. Existing data is preserved; editing is disabled.\";\n    for (const control of form.querySelectorAll<HTMLInputElement | HTMLButtonElement>(\n      \"input, button\",\n    )) {\n      control.disabled = true;\n    }\n    return;\n  }\n\n  function render(): void {\n    pantry.replaceChildren();\n    shopping.replaceChildren();\n    for (const item of list.items()) {\n      const row = document.createElement(\"li\");\n      const label = document.createElement(\"label\");\n      const checkbox = document.createElement(\"input\");\n      checkbox.type = \"checkbox\";\n      checkbox.checked = item.needed;\n      checkbox.setAttribute(\"aria-label\", `${item.name} needed`);\n      label.append(checkbox, document.createTextNode(` ${item.name}`));\n      checkbox.addEventListener(\"change\", () => {\n        mutate(() => list.markNeeded(item.name, checkbox.checked));\n      });\n      row.append(label);\n      pantry.append(row);\n      if (item.needed) {\n        const needed = document.createElement(\"li\");\n        needed.textContent = item.name;\n        shopping.append(needed);\n      }\n    }\n    if (!pantry.childElementCount) pantry.append(emptyRow(\"No pantry items yet.\"));\n    if (!shopping.childElementCount) shopping.append(emptyRow(\"Nothing marked needed.\"));\n  }\n\n  function mutate(action: () => void): boolean {\n    try {\n      action();\n      message.textContent = \"\";\n      render();\n      return true;\n    } catch (error) {\n      message.textContent = error instanceof Error ? error.message : \"Could not update pantry.\";\n      render();\n      return false;\n    }\n  }\n\n  form.addEventListener(\"submit\", (event) => {\n    event.preventDefault();\n    if (mutate(() => list.add(input.value))) input.value = \"\";\n    input.focus();\n  });\n  render();\n}\n\nfunction emptyRow(text: string): HTMLLIElement {\n  const row = document.createElement(\"li\");\n  row.textContent = text;\n  return row;\n}\n\nstart();\n'\"''',\n'src/style.css':'''body {\n  margin: 0;\n  background: #faf9f5;\n  color: #26352a;\n  font: 1rem/1.5 system-ui, sans-serif;\n}\nmain {\n  max-width: 40rem;\n  margin: auto;\n  padding: 2rem 1rem;\n}\nform {\n  display: flex;\n  flex-wrap: wrap;\n  align-items: center;\n  gap: 0.75rem;\n}\ninput, button {\n  font: decode(raw: string | null): Item[] {\n  if (raw === null) return [];\n  const value: unknown = JSON.parse(raw);\n  if (\"'!Array.isArray(value)) throw new Error(\"Invalid saved pantry.\");\n  const accounts or stock tracking.\\n+See [VISION.md](VISION.md), [GLOSSARY.md](GLOSSARY.md) and [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md).\\n+\\n+## Setup and use\\n+\\n+Use Node.js 24 and npm.\\n+Run `npm ci`, then `npm run dev` and open http://127.0.0.1:5173.\\n+The local development server serves browser files; there is no application backend.\\n+Keep that address and browser profile stable: local storage belongs to the origin.\\n+Clearing browser data removes pantry records; this version has no backup or synchronization.\\n+\\n+Add Rice, mark it needed, see Rice in Shopping list, then reload and retain it.\\n+Unchecking Needed removes it from the shopping list while retaining the pantry item.\\n+\\n+## Verification\\n+\\n+Run `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run compile` and `npm run build`.\\n+Install the test browser with `npm run browser:install`, then run `npm test`.\\n+`npm run check` runs all checks and the real browser tests.\\n+Browser tests use an isolated browser profile claim.\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/VISION.md\\n@@ -0,0 +1,23 @@\\n+# Pantry list vision\\n+\\n+## Purpose and responsibility\\n+Pantry list helps a household record pantry supplies and maintain a shopping list of items it needs to buy.\\n+An item is a named pantry supply.\\n+A shopping list contains items the household marks as needing to buy.\\n+The household decides which items are needed; the project records and presents those decisions.\\n+\\n+## Local household use\\n+Version one runs locally in a browser using TypeScript and local storage.\\n+It requires no account, server or remote service.\\n+Recorded items and shopping needs persist after a browser reload.\\n+Changes must preserve this local household model.\\n+\\n+## Focus and boundaries\\n+Accept changes that make recording named pantry items, marking shopping needs and viewing the shopping list clearer and more dependable.\\n+Version one does not track quantities, stock levels or low-stock thresholds, because they add maintenance beyond the simple household marking journey.\\n+Recipe and meal planning remain excluded as separate responsibilities, even when implemented locally.\\n+Accounts, remote synchronization, shared online household services, retailer integration and automatic purchasing are outside version one.\\n+\\n+## Contribution acceptance\\n+A change aligns when it supports household-controlled pantry recording and shopping needs while preserving local browser use and retained data without remote dependencies.\\n+Resist a change when it introduces stock tracking or separate planning storage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(next));\\n+    } catch {\\n+      throw new Error(\\\"Could not save.
